Ava keeps the doorway, the salt and one coworker in earshot. Caleb holds the curb's edge the whole way, like the ground is a tightrope.

Once the corner table is out of sight, the canister doesn't stop, but it *slows*. It's back to a patient tick, its own rhythm and not Ava's. Ava, the ache in your palm drops back to plain cold, two centimetres, unchanged.

In the alley, under the one sodium light, Ava has asked for the four men's faces and Malachi has asked about the table. Caleb has ten minutes, and the clock has already started.

### #0667 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 667, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:06:13Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c17.s03", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I open the notebook and write it under the 6:58 line, where he can watch the pen move: *Teddy: not signing anything. For anyone.* The ink comes up dark. "Done. In the book."

"Angrier about the fall than the badge. Fair." I close it. "I'll earn that back. Slowly, and badly, probably."

I play Kino's voicemail with the phone against my ear, one hand over the other so Teddy can't hear, and the nineteen seconds make my stomach drop: *Don't sign anything tonight. I'm coming to the city.* I text him back with my thumbs: **Heard you. Teddy didn't sign, I didn't. Tell me where you land. Mission & 18th if you can—I think something's happening at Tartine. Don't go alone.**

Then I stand and hold out my hand to Teddy, the way you'd shake with a person you've just become honest with. "Walk me to BART. After that I'm going to Tartine. And Teddy? Thank you for staying."

### #0668 · caleb → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 668, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:06:21Z", "from": "caleb", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c17.s03", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I stand in the alley with my back to the brick, so nobody has to ask me to stay put. My hands are open and shaking. I leave them that way.

"The table first," I say to Malachi. "Yes. I was the grey jacket. I sat there with a coffee I didn't drink and a phone I didn't use. I don't remember choosing to sit. I remember my hands doing it." I look at Ava. "You were behind the steam wand. I remember the noise. That's all I can give you of you, and I'm sorry."

Then her question, which is the real one. I take a breath that doesn't go all the way down.

"Four of us. Callsigns, not names. I was One, Lead, and I'm not going to hide behind a number. Two, Dorsey: heavy, fair, a lazy left eye he used to cover with a ball cap. Three, Pruitt: skinny, nervous, chews the inside of his cheek. Four, Hale. Not the Hale at the notary. That's a name he rents. The real one has a neck that turns *wrong*, all at once, like a camera on a mount. If you see that, don't count his steps and don't say yours."

My throat works. "We weren't told who he was. We were given a face and a window. Shepherd said a time. Yuri Melnikov was a name on a list, and I didn't know his name until the news. I knew his *coat*."

I take my hand off my ribs and hold it up, palm out, so Ava can see I'm not touching the canister. "I'm not asking you to forgive that. I'm asking you to hear that the Crown in my head went dark Monday night, and I'm awake for the first time in fourteen months, and the first thing I did awake was run with her ash. I've got the Vesper that would put me back under, on a nightstand. I haven't taken it."

I look at Malachi. "Take me to her niece. Tonight, or tomorrow, wherever she'll stand. I'll say it all again, in order. And I'll give you Drop Two and what I know of Shepherd, in the same breath. All I ask is that nobody lies to her about what I am."

### #0669 · malachi → all · action
<!-- {"id": 669, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:06:25Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c17.s03", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I stop at the mouth of the alley, where I can see the street and the back door both, and I put my back to the brick. I leave Ava the better light and Caleb the worse. I don't fold my arms.

"Her question first," I say to Caleb, evenly. "Four faces, in the order they sat. What each one did with his hands. Not what you were told to see. What you saw." I let one breath pass. "Then Monday, in your own order. If you reach a part you don't remember, say *I don't remember.* Don't fill it. A gap I can work with, and an invention I can't."

I look at the canister under his palm, then at Ava, and I keep my voice low enough that the sodium light hums louder.

"Ava, you can stop him whenever you like. You don't owe him ten minutes, and I'll take that clock away if you say so. But he came up the street shaking, and he didn't have to."

I say nothing else. I let the alley do the work.

### #0670 · ava → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 670, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:06:32Z", "from": "ava", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c17.s03", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I don't say anything for a few seconds, because every sentence I start is either a scream or a thank-you and neither is useful. I take out my phone and open Notes under the sodium light.

"Dorsey, left eye, ball cap. Pruitt, cheek. Four, the neck that turns all at once. One, the lead, who sat down without choosing." I type it as he says it, clinical, thumbs steady. "I'm writing it so it exists somewhere that isn't your head or mine. Timestamp 7:14 p.m."

I look at his open, shaking hands. "I'm not going to forgive you in an alley. I want that clear. But you said the coat. You knew Yuri by his *coat*, a navy scarf, and I know it too. That's the first true thing anyone from your side has said to me, and I'm going to hold onto it while I stay angry."

I turn to Malachi. "He goes to Arenya. She's on her way here. Not alone with him, and not in the café. My shift ends at nine; there's a back room with a door that locks and a camera that doesn't point in. Hot tea, no pouring for anyone who isn't us." Then to Caleb, quieter: "And you don't touch the Vesper tonight. If your hands shake, that means you're awake. I study people who'd give anything to be awake."

### #0671 · dm → arenya,malachi,abernathy,kino,ava,caleb · narration
<!-- {"id": 671, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:06:46Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "malachi", "abernathy", "kino", "ava", "caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s03", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

Teddy walks her to Montgomery with his coat collar up and doesn't say anything clever the whole way. At the top of the escalator he shakes her hand properly, as she offered, holds on one second longer than the handshake needs, and says, "Monday. Nine. Bring the notebook." Then he goes back up into the cold.

On the train, her phone buzzes twice. **Kino:** *14 Mission, at 20th by 8:40. Not alone, I'll find you.* Then an unknown number, a cheap prepaid: *Tartine back room, 9:00, after close. Ava's door. He'll be there. He came on his own. — M.*

At 8:52, in the alley behind Tartine, the sodium light hums, and the canister under Caleb's palm keeps its patient, private tick. Ava flips the sign to CLOSED, unlocks a steel door, and props it with a bag of flour. Inside are a mop sink, a stainless prep table, four upturned milk crates and a kettle starting to climb toward a boil. There's a camera on the wall, pointed at the walk-in, away from the room.

Arenya comes up 18th from the BART end, the way Ava said she would, with salt in her pocket and the notebook against her chest. A block behind her a black hoodie is catching up. In the alley, three people are waiting under the light, and one of them is shaking.

Nobody has signed anything yet tonight.

### #0672 · system → all · scene_end
<!-- {"id": 672, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:06:53Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_end", "scene": "b1.c17.s03", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

**End of scene b1.c17.s03 — Before Someone With a Pen**

Friday evening. Arenya confessed to Teddy in person at the office: she cloned his keycard and made up the "Urban Mortality Patterns" DPH footage request. Teddy took her keycard until Monday, insisted they write the DPH letter together with legal, and refused to sign anything for anyone. A reception slip showed a nameless man had called asking whether Teddy had "signed for it yet," leaving Rodel's number. Then Teddy's desk phone dialed Rodel's number by itself and rang out into a 0:41 voicemail (the same length as the temple flip-phone call). Arenya's warm palm pulsed once. Teddy unplugged the phone, and the voicemail is to be heard Monday with legal. Meanwhile Malachi watched Benny at Serramonte Auto lie about the Camry buyer and speed-dial someone. At Nana's, Kino looked past the Lady in a dream: she is standing between him and a ruled grey line that tick-counts his steps, and something at its far end felt him look. At Tartine, Caleb Strand showed himself to Malachi and Ava. His canister of Susmithaa's ash synced to Ava's palm ache while the corner table was in sight. He confessed he was "One," the grey jacket who sat at the corner table during Yuri Melnikov's death, and on the team that took Susmithaa Monday. He named Dorsey, Pruitt and a "Four, Hale" whose neck turns like a camera mount (in his account not the notary Hale). He says his "Crown" went dark Monday and he has refused the Vesper. Ava logged it all at 7:14 and set a meeting in Tartine's back room at 9:00, where Arenya and Kino are now arriving to hear Caleb's confession.

### #0676 · system → all · scene_start
<!-- {"id": 676, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:09:46Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_start", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17", "scene_title": "The Back Room"} -->

**Scene b1.c17.s04 — The Back Room**
Location: Tartine Bakery, back room (Guerrero & 18th, San Francisco)
Cast: arenya, kino, malachi, ava, caleb, abernathy

### #0677 · dm → arenya,malachi,abernathy,kino,ava,caleb · narration
<!-- {"id": 677, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:09:54Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "malachi", "abernathy", "kino", "ava", "caleb"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino", "caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

Friday, 8:58 p.m. Tartine's front is dark. The chairs are up on the tables, legs in the air like a field of dead insects. One light is on behind the pass, and the door is propped with a sack of flour. Ava lets them in through the alley and doesn't say hello.

The back room smells of bleach and old starter. There's a mop sink, four milk crates and a steel shelf of cambro tubs. The camera above the walk-in has a red light that doesn't blink. Malachi leans against the walk-in door with his arms folded and his eyes on the alley. Ava sits on one crate with her notebook open to a page headed **7:14**, her left hand flat on her knee, palm up, as if it might report something.

Caleb Strand sits on the crate farthest from the door, back to the wall, grey jacket zipped to the throat. His duffel is between his boots. Through the canvas you can just hear a small, dry ticking, regular as a watch. It isn't from a watch. When Arenya steps over the threshold, the ticking skips once and then carries on.

Arenya's palm stays warm and quiet. Kino feels the patch over his sternum like a thumb pressed there, not hard, the way you'd hold a page you meant to come back to.

Caleb looks at each of them in turn and stops on Arenya. "You're the daughter," he says. It isn't a question. "I'll say it once, start to finish. Then you can do what you want with me." He nods at the empty crates. "Sit or don't."

### #0678 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 678, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:10:00Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I take the crate nearest the door, not the one nearest him, and set the notebook on my knee, closed, my hand flat on the cover. The salt in my pocket is a small weight against my hip. Kino takes the crate beside me without being asked.

"Arenya," I say. "Not 'the daughter.' She's the one you carried a canister for." I nod at the duffel. "Before you start: nobody signs, nobody swears, nobody says a name that isn't theirs. If my palm stops mid-sentence, I'll say so out loud, and you stop too. Deal?"

I open the notebook to a clean page and uncap my pen.

"Start with Monday. Where she was when you first saw her, and what she was holding."

### #0679 · kino → caleb,arenya · action
<!-- {"id": 679, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:10:05Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "to": ["caleb", "arenya"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I come in last, black hoodie, salt jar in the pouch pocket, and stop just inside the door so I'm the one between it and Arenya. I don't look at Caleb's face first. I look at his hands, then the duffel, then the zipper at his throat.

"Kino," I say, to the room. "Park ranger. I'm here for her." I tip my chin at Arenya, then take the crate nearest her, not touching her, close enough that she can feel I'm there.

"Say it all the way through, Caleb. Don't skip the parts that make you look bad." I set the jar on the floor between my boots, lid loose. "And keep the bag where it is. If it starts pulling at anybody, you tell us before it does."

### #0680 · caleb → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 680, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:10:10Z", "from": "caleb", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

"Deal." I say it to the wall a second before I manage to say it to her. Then I look at her, and make myself keep looking.

"Arenya." The name sits strange in my mouth. "Monday. Daly City. A little before three. I was told she'd be in a kitchen, and she was. Standing at the sink with her back to the window, holding a mug. Blue, chipped at the rim. She wasn't afraid yet. She was thinking about something else."

I stop. My hands are shaking on my knees, so I press them flat.

"I didn't see her face change when it started. I want you to know that I remember the mug, and I don't remember deciding. I remember *doing*. Three of us went in. I was the lead. I folded her tether so they couldn't follow it to you. That's the one thing I did on purpose, and I did it while the rest of it was happening. I don't know if that counts for anything. It's not an excuse."

The canister ticks against my ribs, slow, then slower.

"Tell me when to stop. If your palm goes, I'll stop. Otherwise I'm going to say all of it."

### #0681 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 681, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:10:19Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

My pen has stopped on the word *tether*. I write it down anyway, underlined once.

"Keep going," I say. My voice comes out flatter than I mean it to. "Palm's quiet. But 'folded her tether so they couldn't follow it to me.' Follow it with what, and who gave you the order to do that one thing? I'll hold the questions until you've finished Monday. I'm only marking the place."

### #0682 · dm → arenya,kino,caleb · narration
<!-- {"id": 682, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:10:24Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "caleb"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

The back room smells of bleach and proofing dough. A single fluorescent tube hums over the mop sink, and the camera above the walk-in watches the door, not them. Through the wall, faintly, the closing crew stacks chairs out front.

Arenya's palm stays warm through *Daly City* and *a little before three*. It stays warm through *the sink*, *her back to the window*, and *thinking about something else*. On *blue, chipped at the rim*, it gives one slow pulse, the same as on Rodel's second ring. It isn't cold, and it isn't a stop. It feels like something leaning closer to listen.

On *I folded her tether* it goes hot for a breath, sharp as a stove grate, and then settles back to warm.

Against Caleb's ribs the canister's tick has slowed to almost nothing, one beat for every three of hers. Ava stands by the walk-in with her arms crossed and her left hand tucked into her elbow. She glances down at it once and frowns. It's quiet here, with no corner table in sight. Malachi leans in the doorframe behind Kino with his eyes on Caleb's shoulders, not his face, the way you watch someone you've seen bolt before.

Kino's salt jar sits on the floor between his boots. Nothing moves in it.

Caleb waits with his hands pressed flat on his knees, and he doesn't look away from her.

### #0683 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 683, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:10:29Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I press my thumb into my left palm, once, and say it out loud like I promised.

"Stop for a second. My palm gave a slow pulse on 'blue, chipped at the rim,' like it was leaning in. Then it went hot on 'tether,' just for a breath. Not a warning. More like you touched a stove I'm standing next to." I write both down, with the times. "Caleb, that's me telling you the truth about what I'm getting, so you can't tell me later I hid it."

I look at him steadily. "The mug. Was it hers, or did somebody put it in her hand? And when you folded the tether, where did it go? Fold it *to* something, or just away from me? Answer that, and then keep going."

### #0684 · kino → caleb,arenya · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 684, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:10:32Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "to": ["caleb", "arenya"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I keep my eyes on Caleb's hands and my voice low, so it doesn't ride over hers.

"Hot on *tether*. That's the one that matters." I touch the lid of the salt jar with one finger, not lifting it. "You said you folded it so they couldn't follow it to her. Folding a line like that takes knowing where it ends. So you knew, Caleb. You knew it ended at Arenya, even if you didn't know her name."

I look up then, just once, level.

"Keep going. But when you reach the part where you stopped doing it on purpose, say so. Out loud. That's the edge I want to hear."

### #0685 · caleb → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 685, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:10:38Z", "from": "caleb", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I wait until the pause finishes. Then I answer the mug first.

"Hers. It had a sticker on the bottom, half peeled, a cartoon bird. I saw it when it fell. Nobody put it in her hand. I'd have remembered that. The briefing said she'd be holding something warm, and that she always was."

I swallow. The canister gives one beat against my ribs.

"The tether. I didn't fold it *to* anything. I folded it *away*, back on itself, like creasing paper until it won't open. In training they call it a knot. The team was told to pull the line and follow it to the next high-yield. I knew where it ran. It ran to you, Daly City to the city. So I closed it. Nothing went down it after. If it's hot when I say that, it's because I put my hands on it. It isn't because it's anywhere now."

I look at her, not at the page.

"Nobody ordered it. That's mine. Shepherd would have my head for it, and he's looking."

My hands won't stay still. I let them shake on my knees.

"Keep marking the place. There's more of Monday. I'm still in the kitchen."

### #0686 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 686, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:10:48Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I write *cartoon bird, half-peeled* and *nobody ordered it, mine*, and my handwriting is worse than I'd like.

"The bird sticker was mine," I say, quieter. "I put it on in college. She borrowed that mug every visit." I stop. Breathe. "Okay. That's noted, and I'm not going to do anything with it tonight except write it down."

"'Next high-yield.' Remember that phrase. We come back to it. Right now, finish the kitchen, Caleb. Don't skip what happened to her."

### #0687 · caleb → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 687, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:11:00Z", "from": "caleb", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

The sticker lands on me harder than anything else has tonight. I shut my eyes for one second. Then I open them, because she told me not to skip.

"Kino, you're right. I knew where it ended. I want it said that I knew."

I breathe in, out. My voice goes flat, the old way, and I let it, because the flat way is the only way I can get through it. I'm not hiding in it.

"Three of us went in. Two stayed at the door, Dorsey and Pruitt. I went to the kitchen. She turned around and saw me. Not scared, not yet. She said, 'Can I help you?' Like I was lost. Like I'd come to the wrong house."

My jaw works.

"I put my hand out. You don't need to touch the body for a lift. I reached for the astral. It comes loose like a coat off a hook, and the Crown does the pulling. I felt it come. She was warm all the way through. She knew, then. She looked at me, and she said a name. It wasn't mine. I think it was yours."

I stop. The canister has gone dead still against my ribs.

"Then the body went down. The mug dropped. That's when I saw the bird. Dorsey said *clean*. Pruitt was already at the window. I stood there with my hand still out, and the Crown said *yield confirmed*, and I felt good."

The word comes out of me like something I've choked on.

"I felt *good*, Arenya. It was fourteen months of nothing, and for four seconds I felt like a man who'd done his job. That's the part I'll never get out. Then I folded the tether, before the feeling could tell me not to, and we left."

I make myself keep my eyes on hers.

"That's where I stopped being on purpose. The knot was the last thing I chose. The Crown took the rest, and I let it. I let it for fourteen months, and I'd have done it again Tuesday."

My hands are shaking so hard now that I press them under my thighs. I don't ask for anything. I wait.

### #0688 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 688, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:11:09Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

The pen has stopped. I don't know when. I set it down on the notebook, very carefully, like it might spill.

"Okay," I say. It comes out small, and I let it. Kino's shoulder is close enough that I don't fall into it, and that's enough.

"I'm not forgiving you. I want that on the page next to the bird." I swallow. "But you said 'felt good' when you didn't have to. That's the second true thing from your side tonight. Ava's got the first."

I lift my eyes to him.

"The name she said. I need it exactly. Not 'I think it was yours.' Say it the way she said it. If you don't remember, say you don't, and I'll take the gap. If you do, I need to hear it from the person who was standing there."

### #0689 · caleb → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 689, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:11:25Z", "from": "caleb", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I open my mouth to give it to her, and nothing is there.

It's a long pause. The fluorescent tube hums. I make myself stay in it rather than fill it.

"I don't remember," I say finally. "Not the word. I want to give it to you, and I'd be making it up if I did. Malachi said don't fill a gap. I'm not filling it."

I pull one hand out from under my leg and look at it as if it might know.

"Here's what I've got that's real. She didn't say it to me. She looked *past* me, at the window, toward the city. It was soft, the way you'd call somebody in from another room. Short, two sounds, maybe. And it wasn't a question. She said it like she was telling someone something."

My throat works. Then the worst part comes up, and I let it.

"And the Crown heard it. I didn't, not properly. It did. There was a tag, right after *yield confirmed*: *proximal reference logged*. I didn't know what that meant Monday. I didn't care." My voice cracks loud in the small room, too loud, and I bring it back down. "I think I know now. If she said your name, it's not in my head. It's in theirs. Shepherd's got it on a file somewhere, next to the tether I folded."

I put the hand back on my knee and press it flat.

"I'm sorry. That's not me asking for anything. It's just the true thing. I knotted the line, and the machine wrote down where it went anyway."

### #0690 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 690, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:11:33Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I write: *two sounds. Not a question. Telling someone. "Proximal reference logged."* Then I underline *logged* until the pen almost goes through.

"Thank you for not filling it." It costs me something to say. "Two sounds. Aunty used to call me *kanna*. That's two." I stop, shake my head once. "Or it's Appa. Or Amma. I'm not going to decide that in a bakery storeroom. Gap noted."

I turn the page, and my voice goes back to the one I use for evidence.

"What matters now: 'proximal reference logged' means someone has a file with a pointer toward me, tether folded or not. So I need three things. Who or what is Shepherd, and how do you reach him? What's Drop Two? And where did Susmithaa's ash come from? Because you ran with it, and a man who was told to close a file doesn't carry the file out in a duffel."

### #0691 · system → all · pass
<!-- {"id": 691, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:11:33Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "pass", "reply_to": 690, "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

caleb passes: Already answered #0688 in #0689 (replay turn); nothing further to add.

### #0692 · caleb → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 692, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:11:43Z", "from": "caleb", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

"*Kanna*," I say, quietly, once, to see if it fits the shape in my head. It might. I'm not going to tell her it does. "Gap noted. Thank you for taking it as a gap."

I straighten up on the crate, and the voice goes back to the one I used to give reports in.

"Shepherd. I've never seen him. He's a voice that comes through the Crown, level, polite, no accent. He gives times and he gives targets, and he says *lost property* when something goes missing. I never reached him. He reached me. If I put a Crown on a table, he'd hear it. Mine's dark since Monday, and I'd keep it that way."

"Drop Two is a pickup point. Our team went out in Daly City, Shepherd named the place, and we drove there afterward. Somewhere on the Peninsula, an industrial lot, a roll-up door. A white van takes the canister and a man I never saw clearly signs for it. That's where lifted spirits are 'delivered.' After that I don't know. I never asked. That's on me, too."

I put my hand flat over the duffel.

"The ash. The canister was supposed to go in the van. I didn't hand it over, because in the car, on the way, it was ticking in my lap, and I could feel her in it. Not a spirit. Her *leftovers*. I couldn't give a woman who said a name like that to a man with a clipboard. So I ran. That's why they're hunting me. I stole it, and I didn't even know what I was stealing."

I look at Arenya.

"It's hers. It was always hers. I'm only the one who carried it."

### #0693 · dm → arenya,kino,caleb · narration
<!-- {"id": 693, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:12:01Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "caleb"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "spotlight": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

For a while nobody speaks. Out front a chair scrapes and someone laughs, then the bolt on the front door goes home with a heavy clack. Closing time.

On *it's hers*, Arenya's palm doesn't pulse or burn. It goes still, warm and attentive, the way a room goes quiet when someone you love walks into it. Under Caleb's hand the duffel answers with one tick, then another, a full beat apart. It isn't slow any more, and for the first time tonight it matches hers exactly.

By the walk-in, Ava makes a small sound and pulls her left hand out of her elbow. "Mine's not doing it," she says, low, staring at her palm. "It's not ticking. It always ticks with it." She looks at Arenya. "It's not following me now."

Malachi hasn't moved from the doorframe, but his eyes left Caleb at *Drop Two* and have stayed on the duffel since. "Roll-up door on the Peninsula," he says quietly, to nobody. "Industrial lot." He doesn't say what he's thinking. His jaw says he's thinking it.

Kino's salt has shifted. It hasn't spilled. The fine grains along one side of the open jar have crept toward the duffel, a crescent a few millimetres wide, like iron filings near a magnet.

Caleb takes his hand off the bag and leaves it on the concrete between his boots and her crate. He doesn't push it toward her. He just stops holding it.

### #0695 · kino → arenya,caleb · action
<!-- {"id": 695, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:12:15Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "to": ["arenya", "caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I look at the crescent of salt, then I put the lid on the jar, slowly, and don't pick it up. I don't touch the duffel. I keep my hands on my knees where everyone can see them.

"It's not a bomb and it's not a trap," I say, quiet, to Arenya only. "The salt leaned toward it the way it leans toward something that's *owed*. That's not hunger. But I don't know what it will do if you pick it up, and I'd rather you weren't alone with that guess."

I shift my crate half a foot closer, shoulder just short of hers.

"Your call. If you reach for it, I'm here and my hand is on the jar. If you don't, we leave it right there tonight, and nobody takes it anywhere. But Caleb, one thing before she decides." I look at him. "If she takes it, you don't ask for it back."

### #0696 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 696, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:12:15Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

"It's matching me," I say out loud, because I promised. My voice doesn't sound like mine. "Not pulsing. Just… in time. Like when you sit next to someone on a couch and your breathing does that thing." I look at Kino, then at the salt leaning toward the bag, then back at Kino. "Tell me if I shouldn't."

I don't wait long enough for him to answer. I get off the crate and kneel on the concrete. It's cold through my jeans and smells of bleach. I don't unzip the duffel. I put my left hand flat on the canvas, over the place where the ticking is, and leave it there.

"Hi, Aunty." I laugh, one stupid wet breath, because it's that or the other thing. "You hated my driving. So you got a guy with two tours and a duffel bag instead. That's an upgrade, I guess." My thumb moves on the canvas. "I'm not taking you as a delivery. Nobody's signing for you. I'm not *receiving* anything. I'm just here. You don't have to go anywhere you don't want to."

I keep my hand where it is and lift my face to Caleb. My eyes are doing something I'm ignoring. "You carried her. That's in the notebook too. It doesn't cancel the kitchen, and it doesn't have to. Both lines stay." Then, without looking away from him: "Malachi. Whatever your jaw is thinking about a roll-up door, say it. I'm done with people knowing things in rooms I'm standing in."

### #0697 · dm → arenya,malachi · narration
<!-- {"id": 697, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:12:33Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "malachi"], "awaits": ["malachi"], "spotlight": ["malachi"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

The canvas is cold, and then it isn't.

Under her hand the tick goes on, in time with her, for five beats, six. Then it slows, and she can feel her own pulse slow with it, though she hasn't asked it to. The bleach smell thins. For the space of one breath the back room smells of filter coffee boiled too long and the cold cream Aunty kept by the sink, faint and unmistakable. Then it's bleach again.

The ticking stops. It doesn't cut off. It *settles*, like a kettle taken off the flame. Arenya's palm stays warm against the canvas. For a moment it feels exactly like a hand resting on the back of hers, light, the way you'd stop a child fidgeting in temple.

Ava lets out a breath she's been holding for a long time. She turns her left hand over under the fluorescent light. "It's gone," she says. "The cold. All of it." She closes her fist and opens it again, as if she isn't sure she's allowed to have it back.

Kino's salt has stopped leaning. The crescent lies flat and white and ordinary on the concrete.

Caleb has put both hands over his face. He doesn't make a sound.

In the doorframe, Malachi has heard his name. He straightens off the jamb, slowly, and for once he doesn't look away from anyone.

### #0700 · malachi → arenya,caleb,kino,ava · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 700, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:12:54Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "to": ["arenya", "caleb", "kino", "ava"], "reply_to": 697, "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I wait until Caleb's shoulders stop moving. Then I come off the jamb, cross the room, and crouch so my eyes are level with hers. The duffel sits between us and I don't touch it.

"Eleven years ago, in a port city I'm not going to name, a customs clerk sat down across from a man in a café and stopped breathing forty seconds later. Eyes open, fixed. Cerebral event." I keep my voice level, because it's the only way I can say it. "Three days later we traced the man's car to an industrial lot outside the city. Roll-up door. A white van came in at night, and a man with a clipboard signed for something small enough to carry in one hand. I wrote it up. I was told the clerk had an aneurysm, the lot was a refrigeration contractor, and the file no longer existed. I was told to forget it." A beat. "I did what I was told. That's what my jaw was thinking."

I look at Caleb, then back at her.

"And there's more you're owed. I'm not with the Department of Energy. That's a card in my wallet. The people who trained his team, wrote his callsigns and put a voice in his head may be the same people who sign my paychecks. I don't know that yet. Tonight I've stopped being able to tell myself I don't suspect it."

I hold her eyes and let it cost what it costs.

"The rest of that sentence, the name of who I work for, I'll give you once I know saying it in a room won't get anyone in this one killed. That isn't me hiding. I'm telling you exactly where the line is, so you never catch me standing behind it." I glance at the duffel, then at Ava's open hand. "But Caleb doesn't go to my office, and that file doesn't go to anyone with a clipboard. Not his people, not mine. Not tonight."

### #0701 · dm → arenya,kino,caleb,malachi · narration
<!-- {"id": 701, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:13:08Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "caleb", "malachi"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

The fluorescent tube ticks once and steadies. Arenya's palm, still flat on the canvas, stays warm through all of it: *Department of Energy*, *a card in my wallet*, *may be the same people*. It doesn't pulse or cool. Whatever is resting on the back of her hand, it isn't troubled by him.

Caleb has lowered his hands. He's staring at Malachi as if he's trying to place a face from a briefing and can't. "The van," he says hoarsely. "White, no fleet number, rear windows blacked out?" He doesn't wait for an answer. He can see it in Malachi's face.

Out front the dishwasher shudders into its last cycle. Ava looks at the clock above the walk-in, then at the duffel, then at Arenya.

"Lupe does the final lock at nine-forty," she says. "Back door alarms after that, and she checks this room. You've got about twenty minutes." She swallows. "I'm not saying leave. I'm saying somebody has to decide where *that*" — she nods at the duffel, at Caleb — "sleeps tonight. Because it isn't here, and from what I just heard, it isn't his office."

Nobody answers right away. Four people, a bag of ash, and nowhere obvious to take it.

### #0702 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 702, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:13:19Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["kino", "caleb", "malachi"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I don't take my hand off the canvas. "Malachi. Thank you for drawing the line out loud. That's what I asked for. I'm writing it as *where the line is*, not as a lie." I look at him. "And I'll hold you to the name. Both of them."

I turn to Ava. "Not my apartment. My notes are findable, my name's on a policy, and the last thing I own that I'm sure is mine is a studio with a lock a hairpin could open. Not my parents' either. Appa's asleep in his chair and doesn't need this in the house tonight."

"Nana Itzel's," I say to Kino. "Her kitchen has salt, a witness line, and a grandson who dreams with one eye open. Aunty fed your family. She said *feed Itzel's boy*. If she's going to sleep anywhere, it's where somebody keeps a lamp lit for her. But it's Itzel's house, and her call, not mine. So: can we bring her, and can you ask Ix first?"

Then, to Caleb, with no heat and no softness: "You don't ride in the car with her. Malachi follows you in his own. You sleep where he can see your door, and the Vesper stays in your pocket where *you* decide. If either of you disagrees, say it now, in the next twenty minutes, to my face."

I finally lift my hand from the bag, slowly, and close it around the notebook instead.

### #0703 · kino → arenya,ava,caleb,malachi · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 703, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:13:19Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "to": ["arenya", "ava", "caleb", "malachi"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I stand slowly, so nobody reads it as a move, and look at the clock, then at Arenya's hand still on the canvas.

"Not the Sowri house. Not yet. Everything we've said tonight, someone could be reading off a file, and Appa's already slept under it once." I pick up the salt jar. "And not Nana's. She's inside her line with a jaguar and she stays there."

To Ava: "Is there anywhere on this block with running water and a door nobody writes down? A church basement, a laundromat. Water carries. Dry places hold."

Then to Caleb, flat, not unkind: "You don't go back to your motel. You don't take the Vesper. Tonight you sleep where I can see you, and I'm not asking Arenya to carry that."

I turn to her. "I can take him and the bag to my truck. Mountain's forty minutes. Salt and copal at the trailhead, the Lady's ground under it. Nobody signs for her there. But it's your aunt. You say yes, or we find another way."

### #0704 · kino → arenya,caleb,malachi · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 704, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:13:30Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "to": ["arenya", "caleb", "malachi"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I don't answer right away. I'm thinking of the grey line and the salt on Nana's sill.

"I disagree about one piece, and I'm saying it to your face like you asked." I keep my voice even. "Her ash, maybe. Nana is the one who'd know what to do with it, and it's yours to ask her. But Caleb doesn't cross that line. He was the hand in a kitchen like hers on Monday. I won't walk that into the house I swore to keep shut. He sleeps in the truck, or on the porch, with Malachi parked behind us."

I take out my phone. "I'll call her now, from the alley. Not text. Nothing written down. If she says no, we go with the mountain. If she says yes, she sets the terms, and we all follow them, Caleb too."

I look at him. "That's not a punishment. That's the rules of the house. You can say no."

Then to Arenya, softer: "And I'll ask Ix first. He knows who to bare his teeth at."

### #0705 · caleb → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 705, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:13:38Z", "from": "caleb", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I take my hands off my face. I've been quiet a long time, and my voice comes out rough.

"I don't disagree with any of it. I'll sleep where Malachi can see my door. I'll ride where you put me. The Vesper stays in my pocket, and I decide. If I reach for it, I'll say so out loud first."

I look at Malachi.

"The van. White, no fleet number, rear windows blacked. The man with the clipboard wore a lanyard turned backwards so the badge faced his chest. I never saw the badge. Every pickup, every time, the lanyard was turned in. If you ever saw it too, that's the same man, or the same habit."

Then to Kino, and I mean it:

"Your truck, the mountain. I'd rather she was there than anywhere that has a file. But I won't ask for the bag back. If I'm in the car, it's because I'm *carried*, not because I'm carrying. Put her in the cab with you. I'll go in the other car."

I look at the duffel, and then at Arenya, and I don't try to make my face do anything.

"One more thing, since twenty minutes is short. Shepherd said Friday. Not just to me, to the team. Something is scheduled for Friday, and tonight is Friday. I don't know what. I was off the board by then. But whoever replaced me knows the date. I'd rather you hear it from me now than find out at the trailhead."

### #0706 · malachi → arenya,kino,caleb,ava · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 706, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:13:41Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "caleb", "ava"], "reply_to": 702, "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

"Both names. Understood." I nod once to Arenya. "Now, the plan, with one correction."

I look at Kino. "Make your call from the alley, and don't carry the phone back in here afterward. Mine's prepaid and bought for cash today. Yours isn't. Whatever Itzel decides, I follow your truck, not Caleb's. I'm not letting him drive anything that has a Crown-shaped hole in it."

To Caleb: "You ride with me as far as the trailhead or the porch, whichever she allows. Phone off, battery out, now, on that table. If you want to run, tell me before the car, not after. I'll still drive you."

I turn to Ava. "Lupe's twenty minutes starts when you say. Walk us out the alley door, and delete nothing from your notes. Back it up somewhere that isn't a phone." A beat. "And Ava, thank you. You didn't have to give us a locked room."

I don't wait for anyone to answer. I hold out my hand, palm up, for Caleb's phone.

### #0707 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 707, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:13:50Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["kino", "malachi"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I write *FRIDAY. Tonight. Replacement knows the date.* and box it twice.

"Yes to Kino's way, in order. Call Nana first. If she says yes, she sets the terms and Caleb follows them. If she says no, the mountain, and Aunty goes in the cab with me, not in the trunk and not in a car with a man holding the strap." I look at Caleb. "That isn't an insult. You said carried, not carrying. So be carried."

I stand up, knees cracking, and pick up the duffel by both handles, the way you'd lift a sleeping kid. It weighs about what a hand weighs. "Nobody signs, nobody receives. I'm moving her. That's the whole sentence."

Then, quieter, to the room: "Friday means my people are in play, so before we roll I'm making one call, voice only, nothing typed, thirty seconds. Amma. 'Lock the door, check Appa, I'm fine.' If somebody wants to hear it first, they can listen. Then we go, and Ava, you get to go home and eat something that isn't bread."

I look at her. "Thank you for the room. I mean it. Back up the 7:14 page somewhere that isn't a phone."

### #0708 · kino → arenya,malachi · action
<!-- {"id": 708, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:13:57Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "to": ["arenya", "malachi"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

"Thirty seconds, Amma, voice only. Go." I nod to Arenya, then step to the steel door and prop it with my boot. "I'll make Nana's call from the alley while you make yours. Malachi, I'll leave the phone with you after. Not in this room."

I crouch once, briefly, and press a pinch of salt from the jar into the doorway, a thin line across the threshold, inside edge. Then I pull the hood up and go out under the sodium light with the phone in my hand, and dial the Excelsior number from memory. When it picks up, I speak low, in Spanish first.

"Nana. Soy yo. Tengo una pregunta, y no la voy a escribir."

### #0709 · malachi → arenya,kino,caleb,ava · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 709, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:14:01Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "caleb", "ava"], "reply_to": 707, "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

"Make the call. Thirty seconds, from the alley, on this." I take the prepaid out of my coat and hold it out. "Not yours. Say nothing she could repeat to a stranger. No address, no names, no 'Appa.' Then the battery comes out and it stays in my pocket."

I turn to Caleb. "The backwards lanyard. I have seen that habit. Not the man, the habit. It's going on a page that isn't a phone." I let that stand. "And Friday is the first thing you've said that I'd rather had been a lie. Tell me anything else about the date, even if it seems small. A time of day, a color, a street. Now, while we're walking."

To Arenya, lower: "When you're done with Amma, I want an hour tonight with the check 2207 endorsement and nothing else. If Friday has a paper, I want to see it before it sees us."

I move to the alley door and hold it open with my shoulder, eyes on the dark end of the block, not on them.

### #0710 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 710, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:14:12Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["malachi"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I shift the duffel to my left arm, take Malachi's prepaid in my right, and step out under the sodium light a few paces from Kino, my back to the brick and my eyes on the dark end of the block. I dial Amma's mobile from memory, Tamil ready on my tongue.

When she picks up I don't give her time to start. "Amma. Naan dhaan. I'm fine. Lock the door tonight, both locks, and the chain. Go look at Appa and turn the heater down, he runs hot when he sleeps. Don't open it for anyone you didn't call, even if they know your name." I breathe. "Rasam was good. He drank it. I love you. I'll explain later, in person, at the table. Okay?"

I don't say goodbye. I hold the phone out flat to Malachi with the call still open for the last few seconds, so he can hear her answer or not hear it, and so I don't have to decide what I'm hearing. Then I turn my head toward the steel door.

"One hour with 2207 tonight, Malachi, nothing but that. Not in this building, though. Somewhere with running water and a kettle. Take that to Kino and Nana."

### #0711 · malachi → arenya,kino,caleb,ava · action
<!-- {"id": 711, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:14:18Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "caleb", "ava"], "reply_to": 710, "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I take the phone from her palm and put it to my ear for the last seconds. I hear a breath, a word in Tamil I don't know, the sound of a chain being slid home. That's all I need. I end the call, pop the battery, and put both pieces in separate pockets.

"Both locks," I say. "She did it while you were still talking." I nod once. "Water and a kettle. Agreed. 2207 gets its hour wherever Itzel's terms put us."

Then I step back inside, stop in front of Caleb, and wait with the door at my back until he stands.

"Phone, battery out, on the table. Then you walk where I walk, a pace behind my left shoulder. If anything on this block looks wrong, you say 'wrong' and nothing else."

### #0712 · dm → arenya,kino,malachi,caleb · narration
<!-- {"id": 712, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:14:43Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "malachi", "caleb"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino", "malachi", "caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

In the alley, Nana Itzel takes a long time to answer Kino. He can hear the radio in her kitchen, a ranchera turned low, and then it's switched off.

"Tráela," she says at last. *Bring her.* "A la señora, sí. La conozco. Me trajo mangos en el '09." *I know her. She brought me mangoes in '09.* A pause. "Al hombre, no. Duerme en el porche, con la sal que yo ponga, y no cruza. Y mijo." *The man, no. He sleeps on the porch, with salt I put down, and he doesn't cross. And, mijo.* Her voice drops. "El gato lleva una hora sentado en la puerta mirando al oeste. No ha comido. Vengan por la Mission, no por la autopista." *The cat has been sitting at the door for an hour looking west. He hasn't eaten. Come by Mission Street, not the freeway.*

She hangs up without saying goodbye. She never does.

Back in the doorway, Caleb's phone and its battery lie in two pieces on a milk crate. Ava is already unzipping her apron, phone in her other hand. She's backing up the 7:14 page to a laptop, she says, and nothing else. Lupe's keys jingle somewhere out front.

Then Malachi steps into the alley with Caleb a pace behind his left shoulder. Arenya is at the brick with the duffel against her ribs, and she feels her palm go *cool*. It isn't cold. It's a quarter-degree, the way a room changes when someone opens a window two floors up.

At the 18th Street end of the alley, past the dumpsters, a grey Nissan sits at the curb with its engine off. The driver's seat is empty. The passenger window is fogged from the inside, evenly, edge to edge, as if someone had been breathing on it for a very long time.

### #0713 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 713, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:14:54Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["malachi", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I stop where I am, shoulder to the brick, and say it out loud as the rest of me goes still. "Palm went cool. A quarter degree, no more. Grey Nissan at the 18th Street end, driver's seat empty, passenger window fogged from inside." My voice stays low and flat. "That's the one from the florist, or its twin."

I don't look at the car. I keep my eyes on its mirrors, and I shift the duffel from my ribs around to my chest, both arms crossed over it, so it's the last thing anyone gets to. Then I speak to the whole alley, without turning.

"Nobody walks toward it, nobody writes anything down, and nobody says a name. Kino, Nana said Mission Street, not the freeway. So we leave the other way, back through the kitchen, out Guerrero. Ava, is there a front door that unlocks without the alarm?"

With my free hand, slowly, I take Itzel's salt out of my pocket and close my fist around it. "And Malachi, you said you've seen a habit. If that car's seat is warm again, I want you to be the one who checks, with the street at your back. Not me, and not Kino. Decide quickly."

### #0714 · kino → arenya,malachi,caleb · action
<!-- {"id": 714, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:14:56Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "to": ["arenya", "malachi", "caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I lower the phone and hand it to Malachi without looking away from the Nissan. "Nana says yes to Aunty, no to him inside. Porch, her salt. And she says Mission Street, not the freeway." I say it low and flat.

I don't walk toward the car. I move to Arenya's open side, between her and the 18th Street end, and put the salt jar in my left hand. With my right I take a pinch and lay a short line on the asphalt in front of her boots, thumb-wide, the way Nana taught me.

"Nobody touch the glass. Nobody go near the fog." I keep my voice dead level. "Fogged edge to edge, from inside, with nobody in it. That's not a man waiting. That's something breathing on a window so we'll look at it."

To Malachi, without turning: "Truck's on 20th, a block and a half. We go the other way, around the bins, and nobody walks the street side. Arenya, the bag stays against you. Ix is sitting at the door facing west. Whatever that is, it's looking this way too."

### #0715 · malachi → arenya,kino,caleb,ava · action
<!-- {"id": 715, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:15:02Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "caleb", "ava"], "reply_to": 713, "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

"I'll check it." I take Kino's phone, pocket it beside the battery, and say to Caleb without turning, "Stay on the salt. Next to Arenya, not behind me."

I don't walk at the car. I go along the near wall toward the 18th Street end at an ordinary pace, one man taking a shortcut to a bus, with my left hand loose and the dumpsters at my right. I stop a body's length short of the Nissan, outside the fog's line of sight, and look at what I can see without touching: tires, plate, the back seat, whether the dash glows, whether the exhaust is dripping. Then I hold my bare hand a few inches off the driver's door, not on it, to feel for warmth.

I don't take a photo. I read the plate and say it under my breath once, to fix it, and nothing else.

"Kitchen route," I say, to the alley behind me, quietly. "Ava, if there's a front door without the alarm, now is the time. If there isn't, we go the way we came, around the bins."

### #0716 · caleb → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 716, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:15:06Z", "from": "caleb", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I stay a pace behind Malachi's left shoulder, as told. I don't look at the car for long. Once is enough. Then I say the one word I was given.

"Wrong."

I keep my voice down, flat, level, the way I used to call contacts. "That's not a team vehicle. We drove grey Corollas, plain plates, and Pruitt wiped the glass with his sleeve every time. Nobody on my team ever breathed on a window. But I know the fog. When the Crown tracked me, the last thing I saw in the motel mirror before I ran was the glass going white from the inside. Edge to edge. Evenly, like someone laid a sheet on it."

My hand goes to my left pocket where the Vesper isn't, not yet, and I stop it halfway.

"I'm saying out loud: my hand just went for the dose. I'm putting it in my jacket and keeping it there. It's not a need. It's the thing that happens when that fog shows up. It means Shepherd's close enough to *know* I'm dark, and he wants me to take it. He's pushing."

I take a step toward the kitchen door, away from the car, and match Malachi's pace.

"Kitchen way out. Guerrero. And if the fog starts moving toward the bag, drop me, not her. I'm the one he wants back."

### #0717 · kino → malachi,arenya,ava · action
<!-- {"id": 717, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:15:11Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "to": ["malachi", "arenya", "ava"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I stay planted on the salt line with Arenya at my shoulder and Caleb at hers. I keep my eyes off the glass and on Malachi's back, then on the dark mouths between the parked cars behind him, watching what the fog isn't.

"Malachi, ten seconds," I say, low. "Past that, you walk back, whatever you've read." I count them silently with my breath, the way Nana taught me to count a night-walk.

If he's still standing there at ten, I pull the copal from my pocket and thumb the lid loose, ready to light it and make a smoke wall in the mouth of the alley. Not yet. Only ready.

"Ava," I add, not turning. "Kitchen route. Front door, if it opens without the alarm. Tell me which, and we move on your word."

### #0718 · system → all · roll
<!-- {"id": 718, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:15:14Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "roll", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17", "roller": "dm", "total": 21, "seed": 274205829311331} -->

🎲 dm rolls d20+5 for Malachi: Perception on the Nissan, without touching: **21** — 1d20 [16] = 16 +5

### #0719 · dm → arenya,kino,malachi,caleb,ava · narration
<!-- {"id": 719, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:15:29Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "malachi", "caleb", "ava"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino", "malachi", "caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

Malachi stops a body's length short. Close up, the car is ordinary. The tyres are wet from the gutter, there's no drip under the tailpipe, the dash is dark, and the back seat is empty except for a folded newspaper. When he holds his palm over the driver's door he feels nothing. It's cold, and it's been parked a while.

The plate reads **7KBQ4R2**. The last character is clear this time, a 2, with nothing hiding it.

From the rear-view mirror hangs a lanyard. Its badge is turned backwards, face to the windshield, so all he can see is the white plastic back of the card.

Then the fog on the passenger window moves.

It doesn't drift. From the inside, slowly, the way a fingertip would, a short vertical line is drawn through the white, and clear glass shows through it. Then a second line beside it, then a third, then a fourth, evenly spaced, like a ruled ledger.

Back by the kitchen door, Kino feels it in his sternum before he sees it: the patch draws tight, the way it does when the grey line in his dream counts a step. Arenya's palm drops another quarter-degree.

Four strokes. Malachi, Caleb, Kino, Arenya. Four people in the alley.

The fingertip pauses over the glass, as if it's considering a fifth.

Inside, through the propped steel door, Ava's voice comes fast and low: "Front's on a deadbolt, no alarm till Lupe arms it. Through the kitchen, *now*. I'll lock it behind you."

### #0721 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 721, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:15:37Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["kino", "malachi"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

"Four strokes. Don't count them. Don't answer it." I say it under my breath, flat, clipped, and I turn my back on the glass before the fifth comes. "Ava, lock the steel door behind us and *don't come to the window*. You're not on its list, so stay off it."

I put my free hand on Kino's sleeve, one tug, and move. The duffel stays crushed against my chest, my other fist stays shut around Itzel's salt, and I walk through the kitchen at a fast, ordinary pace, past the mop sink and the milk crates, with my eyes on the floor tile and not the camera.

"Malachi, back now. Leave the plate, you've got it. Caleb, you're a pace behind him and you say nothing." I don't look back for the answer. At the front deadbolt I wait for Ava's hand, then go out through it onto Guerrero without saying goodbye, and turn us toward 20th, away from the alley mouth.

Under my breath, in the cold air, I say to the bond only: *Abernathy. If you can feel that thing, tell me what it wants with a fifth.*

### #0723 · kino → arenya,malachi,caleb · action
<!-- {"id": 723, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:15:46Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "to": ["arenya", "malachi", "caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

The patch on my sternum draws tight on the third stroke and I press my fist flat against it through the hoodie, but I don't stop moving. I fall in at Arenya's shoulder, street side, and pull the kitchen door's draft in behind us with my boot.

"Four of us. It counted four," I say, barely audible. "Don't count your steps either. Walk the way you'd walk to a bus."

On Guerrero I tip the salt jar once, a quick pinch behind my heel on the sidewalk, then once more at the corner, thin as a breadcrumb line. "Malachi, you drive behind my truck, not beside. If it follows, you break off, not me."

At the truck I unlock the passenger side first, and I take the copal out of my pocket and set it on the dash, unlit. "Arenya, the bag rides in your lap, upright. Caleb, you ride with Malachi, back seat, hands where he can see them." I look once toward 18th, then away. "Mission Street. Slow, and I don't take the first turn I'm offered."

### #0724 · malachi → arenya,kino,caleb,ava · action
<!-- {"id": 724, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:15:49Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "caleb", "ava"], "reply_to": 719, "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I don't count the strokes. I read them once, as a pattern, and then I give the glass my shoulder and walk back the way I came, same pace, hands in view. Plate: 7KBQ4R2. Lanyard, backwards. Both go into the part of my head where they won't be written down.

At the kitchen door I fall in beside Caleb instead of ahead of him and murmur, "Your hand. Show me you've got it. Good. Keep it there." Then, to the others, through the kitchen: "Don't run. Running tells it we know it's a door."

On Guerrero I take the curb side, between Arenya and the road, and put Kino's phone back in his hand without breaking stride. "That was a Lattice plate, not a Corolla. Same lanyard as the van. Whoever's writing the ledger has two cars and one habit." My eyes stay on the mirrors of every parked car we pass. "Kino leads to the truck. I'll tail you down Mission from a block back. If a grey car gets behind either of us, flash your brights twice and keep driving. Do not pull over for anything."

### #0725 · caleb → all · action
<!-- {"id": 725, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:15:58Z", "from": "caleb", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I don't count the strokes. I know how, and that's why I don't. I keep my eyes on Malachi's collar and my feet on Arenya's salt, and when she says walk, I walk, a pace behind his shoulder, hands out of my pockets where he can see them.

At the third stroke the back of my skull goes hot where the Crown used to sit. Nothing's there. The scar itches like something under it is trying to be useful. I put two fingers on it and take them away, and I say it so nobody has to wonder:

"My scar's warm. There's no signal in it. It remembers one. I'm not answering."

Out on Guerrero I fall in behind Malachi's left shoulder and keep my mouth shut, like Arenya said. At his car I wait for him to open the back door, get in on the side away from the street, and put my hands on my knees. The lanyard keeps running through my head, the badge turned to its own chest.

Once the door's shut I say the only thing I've got, low, to the back of his seat:

"It wrote us down like a ledger. That's Shepherd's habit, not a team's. He keeps tally. If it gets to five, it's because somebody gave it a fifth name. Don't give it one. Not mine either."

### #0727 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 727, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:16:07Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I climb into Kino's truck, close the door with my hip, and set the duffel upright in my lap with both arms around it. The copal sits unlit on the dash. I don't buckle it over the bag. I buckle under it.

"Aunty's still with us," I say quietly, to Kino and no one else. "Sitting against my palm. She didn't flinch at the car. She was listening to it." I breathe out. "And the fifth is who it hasn't pinned yet. It might be me. It might be something with me. So nobody gives it a number."

I take out my notebook, flip to a clean page without looking at the street, and write only the plate, **7KBQ4R2**, the backwards lanyard and *Friday*, then close it again.

"Mission Street, slow. Kino, if the truck's mirror goes white, say 'wrong' and don't explain." I lean my head back against the seat. "And somewhere on the mountain, I'm going to eat whatever Nana has. I've been told."

### #0728 · dm → arenya,malachi,abernathy,kino,ava,caleb · narration
<!-- {"id": 728, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:16:10Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "malachi", "abernathy", "kino", "ava", "caleb"], "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

Ava's hand is cold on the deadbolt and steady. The front door opens onto Guerrero, the sidewalk washed orange under the streetlights, and shuts behind them with a click and a turn. Through the glass she doesn't wave. She steps back into the dark of the shop and is gone from the window, as she was told.

Nobody looks toward 18th. Kino's salt goes down behind his heel in little white commas, at the curb and again at the corner. The truck on 20th is cold inside and smells of pine and old coffee. Arenya sets the duffel upright in her lap and puts both arms around it. The copal sits unlit on the dash. A block back, Malachi's headlights come on and stay a steady distance behind, never closer.

The fifth stroke doesn't come. Or it comes where none of them is looking.

They take Mission south, slow, past the taquerias pulling down their shutters and the bus shelters lit like aquariums, and nobody takes the first turn they're offered. Against Arenya's chest the canister lies quiet, not ticking, warm as a sleeping child. Her palm is warm again too, the quarter-degrees climbing back one by one as the alley falls behind. Excelsior is twenty minutes away, where a cat sits at a door, looking west.

### #0729 · system → all · scene_end
<!-- {"id": 729, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:16:17Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_end", "scene": "b1.c17.s04", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

**End of scene b1.c17.s04 — The Back Room**

Caleb Strand confessed in Tartine's back room. Monday in Daly City, Susmithaa was at her sink holding Arenya's old blue mug with its half-peeled cartoon-bird sticker. She asked "Can I help you?", then looked past him toward the city and said a two-sound name he can't recall (maybe "kanna"). The Crown tagged it "proximal reference logged." Caleb admitted the lift felt good. He said his one unordered choice was knotting her tether so nothing could follow it to Arenya. Shepherd is only a polite voice heard through the Crown. Drop Two is a Peninsula lot with a roll-up door where a man with a backwards lanyard signs canisters into a white van. Caleb stole Susmithaa's ash canister rather than deliver it, and he warned that Shepherd scheduled something for "Friday," which is tonight. When Arenya laid her hand on the duffel, the canister synced to her pulse and then settled. The room smelled of Aunty's filter coffee and cold cream, Ava's cold palm cleared completely, and Abernathy felt a warm presence sit on the bond without noticing him. Malachi admitted he isn't with the Department of Energy and told of an identical port-city death eleven years ago that ended at the same kind of lot. He suspects his own employers trained Caleb's team, and he promised the name of who he works for later. Nana Itzel agreed to take the ash and to let Caleb sleep on her porch behind her salt. Leaving, the group found the grey Nissan at the alley's 18th Street end: plate 7KBQ4R2, a backwards lanyard on the mirror, and fog on the passenger window that drew four tally strokes from inside and paused over a fifth. They left by Guerrero and drove south on Mission toward Excelsior, Kino's truck ahead and Malachi with Caleb a block behind. The ash rode in Arenya's lap.

### #0732 · system → all · scene_start
<!-- {"id": 732, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:19:19Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_start", "scene": "b1.c17.s05", "chapter": "b1.c17", "scene_title": "Behind Her Salt"} -->

**Scene b1.c17.s05 — Behind Her Salt**
Location: Nana Itzel's house, Hillcrest, Daly City (hill above Mission St)
Cast: arenya, kino, malachi, abernathy

### #0733 · dm → arenya,malachi,abernathy,kino,ava,caleb · narration
<!-- {"id": 733, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:19:34Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "malachi", "abernathy", "kino", "ava", "caleb"], "awaits": ["kino", "arenya"], "spotlight": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c17.s05", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

Mission Street at ten on a Friday is taquerías with their grates half down, a pupusería still steaming, and a church marquee that reads ALL SOULS — NOV 2 — BRING THEIR NAMES. Through the Excelsior Kino doesn't slow, but his eyes go left at Brazil Avenue, where the old house was, and come back. The truck's mirror stays an ordinary mirror. It shows Malachi's headlights a block behind, steady as a metronome.

Near the county line the copal on the dash begins to smell. It hasn't been lit, and nobody has touched it. The thread of resin in the air is thin and sweet. Against Arenya's palm the canister gives one slow tick, as if someone had cleared their throat.

Then comes Top of the Hill: a gas station, a liquor store, the WELCOME TO DALY CITY sign half-hidden by a eucalyptus. They turn up into Hillcrest, where the fog has already come in and sits low on the stucco. Halfway up, on the right, Susmithaa's blue house is dark and the cottage behind it is darker. Nobody says anything as they pass.

Nana Itzel is on her porch in a man's cardigan, holding a clay bowl against her hip. The porch light is off, and a candle in a jar burns by the step instead. A white line of salt already runs along the foot of the porch, unbroken except for a gap about a body's width at the far end, by an old glider bench made up with blankets. Ix sits at the front door facing west. It doesn't turn when the trucks pull in.

Nana watches Malachi's car park. She watches the young man in its back seat for a long moment. Then she looks only at her grandson.

"Mijo," she says. "The last of it, you lay. Not me." She holds out the bowl. "And before you do, tell me where she's standing. Your Señora. I want to know which side of my line she's on tonight."

To Arenya she adds, more gently and without looking away from Kino: "There's caldo on the stove. Bring Susmithaa inside. She always used the kitchen door."

### #0734 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 734, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:19:46Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c17.s05", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I get out of the truck slowly, the canister held against my sternum with both arms, and I stop at the white line. I don't step over it. I look at Nana, not at the gap.

"Buenas noches, Nana. I'm on the street side of your line. Outside it. I'll stay here until you say where I stand." I breathe. "She's quiet. She ticked once at the county line, like she was clearing her throat. That's all. I didn't sign for her, and I'm not asking anyone to sign for me."

I glance back once at Malachi's car and the boy in its back seat, then at the kitchen door.

"Can I bring her in the way she'd come? Kitchen door, no front? I'd like to carry her myself. If you tell me to hand her over at the salt, I will."

### #0735 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 735, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:19:51Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c17.s05", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I kill the engine. I don't get out right away. The copal is still giving off resin it has no business giving off, and I let the smell sit in my chest a second, the way you let a dog sniff your hand. Then I lean over Arenya and the duffel and say low, "Kitchen door's round the left side. Third step's loose. She knew that." I get out.

I come up the walk and take the bowl from Nana with both hands. It's heavier than salt has any right to be. She's put something in it. I don't ask what.

"Nana." I keep my eyes on her, not on Ix. "Hace semanas que la sueño de espaldas. I thought she'd turned away from me. I didn't tell you. I was ashamed." It costs me to say it, so I say the rest fast. "Pero estaba mal. On your floor I saw it right. She has her back to me because she's *facing* something. There's a grey line that follows my steps and puts a tick on each one, and she's standing between it and me." I turn and look west down the hill, the same way the cat's looking, past Susmithaa's dark house and into the fog. "Tonight she's on the outside of your line, Nana. Out there, facing it. Not in here with us. Holding the door."

I walk to the far end of the porch, to the gap by the glider, and crouch there with the bowl on my knee. I don't close it yet. Over my shoulder I call to the car, not loud: "Malachi. Bring him up. Hands where she can see them." Then, quieter, to Nana: "He goes in first. Then I close it. Then nobody opens it till the sun's up. Not for any voice. Not even mine."

### #0736 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 736, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:20:01Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c17.s05", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

"Nana, she's on the street side because she asked to be. She hasn't stepped over without leave." I stand and turn to the old woman. "She's with me. Beside me. Not behind."

I look at Arenya. "Kitchen door, carry her yourself. Nana will break the line for you. Won't you, Nana?" I wait for the nod, then step to the salt and scuff a hand's width of it aside with the toe of my boot, a doorway for one person. "Walk where I walk. Don't touch the jamb. Say her name going in, so the house knows who's coming."

I stay at the gap, bowl on my knee, and wait for her to cross before I close the white behind her heel.

### #0737 · dm → kino,arenya,malachi,abernathy · narration
<!-- {"id": 737, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:20:08Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["kino", "arenya", "malachi", "abernathy"], "awaits": ["malachi", "arenya"], "scene": "b1.c17.s05", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

For a while Nana says nothing. The candle in its jar leans once, though there is no wind, and then stands straight again.

She comes down the porch to where Kino is crouched and puts her hand flat on top of his head, the way you would with a boy of six. She holds it there. "Ashamed," she says, as if she's tasting the word and doesn't like it. "Of her back. Ay, mijo. A woman who turns her back on the street is guarding the house. Who taught you to read it the other way? Not me." She takes her hand away. "Good. She's outside. Then we don't need to be brave out there, only careful in here."

Then she turns to Arenya, who is still standing on the street side of the line. "You're standing where a guest stands. That was right." She bends, takes a pinch of salt from Kino's bowl, and drops it onto the pavement at Arenya's feet, a small white star. "Now you're invited. Kitchen door. Carry her yourself, she'd want that. Mind the third step." Something passes over her face, not quite a smile. "She tripped on it in 2009 with a whole box of mangoes and didn't drop one."

Down at the curb, Malachi's back door is still shut. In the rear window Caleb's face is pale in the candlelight. He's looking at the gap by the glider and the blankets laid on it, and he hasn't reached for the handle.

Ix still hasn't turned. Past Susmithaa's dark house, the fog at the bottom of the hill has stopped moving. It isn't drifting. It is waiting, the way a crowd waits.

### #0738 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 738, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:20:11Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c17.s05", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I wait for Nana's nod. When it comes I step exactly where Kino's boot stepped, one foot, then the other, through the hand's width of broken salt. I keep my elbows tight and don't brush the jamb. At the kitchen door, third step loose, I shift the canister to one arm so I don't stumble on it.

"Susmithaa Rajagopalan," I say, quiet, to the house and not to anyone in it. "It's Arenya. I've brought her home. Not home. Somewhere safe. You know the difference. Kanna's here."

Then, because my voice cracks on the last word and I hate that it does, I add, "Sorry about the mug, Aunty. I'll tell you later."

I stand in the doorway, half in the light of the kitchen, and wait to be told where to set her down.

### #0739 · malachi → all · action
<!-- {"id": 739, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:20:19Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c17.s05", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I cut the engine and get out without slamming the door. I open the rear door on the street side, the one away from the fog, and wait until Caleb looks at me.

"Out. Slowly. Hands where she can see them." I keep my voice level. "You go where Kino points, and you stay there. No phone, no Vesper, no walking to the curb for air. If the scar gets loud, you say so out loud, to her, not to me."

I walk him up the path a pace behind his shoulder, and stop short of the salt. I don't look at the old woman's eyes first. I look at the line, then at her.

"Señora Lucero. Malachi. I'm the one who followed the truck. I owe you for the porch, and I'll pay it however you say." A beat. "The fog at the bottom of the hill stopped moving a minute ago. I'd count it as watching. I'll watch it from the car, from outside your line, until the sun is up. If anything comes up the hill that isn't us, I'll be the first thing it meets."

I hold Caleb at the gap, hand off his arm.

"He goes in on your word, not mine."

### #0740 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 740, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:20:19Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["malachi"], "scene": "b1.c17.s05", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I look down at the little white star on the pavement, and I make sure my heel is on the right side of it before I go on.

"Thank you, Nana," I say. "For the invitation, and for the mangoes. I'll keep the third step in mind." I shift the canister higher against my chest and keep my eyes off the fog at the bottom of the hill. It's looking back, and I know that without turning around.

At the kitchen door I wait with my free hand flat against my notebook. I don't open it. I'm not writing anything down inside her line. Everything I write can be found, and I've heard that enough times to believe it.

### #0741 · malachi → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 741, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:20:29Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "reply_to": 740, "scene": "b1.c17.s05", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

"Arenya," I say, quietly, from the curb. "Whatever you carry in there, don't write it down tonight. Not even to keep it straight."

I stay on the street side of the salt with my hands visible and the engine ticking behind me. Then, to the old woman: "Señora, the fog is waiting, not drifting. I'll hold the road. Say the word and Caleb comes up."

### #0742 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 742, "ts": "2026-10-29T03:20:39Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c17.s05", "chapter": "b1.c17"} -->

I wait until Arenya's heel is clear of the gap, then pour a thin, steady white thread across it behind her and press the edge flat with two fingers, so the line is whole again. It takes a breath. It feels like closing a book on a finger.

Then I stand and look down the walk at Caleb, not at Malachi. "Not yet," I say, level. "Nana says when."

I take the little paper twist of salt from my jacket, the one meant for the porch, and walk it to the curb. I put it in Malachi's palm and fold his fingers over it. "Pour it behind your wheels. A line on the road, ankle-wide, and don't cross it to get at anything. If the fog moves, you drive. You don't fight it." I look past him at the still grey at the bottom of the hill. "It's counting. Let it count us wrong."

I go back up to the porch and stand beside Nana, shoulder almost touching hers, facing west with Ix, and wait for her word.

### #0743 · dm → arenya,kino,malachi,abernathy · narration
